\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\psi
.

Proof.

Let
x
and
y
be two different element variables. We give the following counterexamples.

(i)

Let us prove that
x\vee y\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}x\wedge y
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be arbitrary.
Then
\mathcal{A}\vDash x\vee y
iff (for every
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(x\vee y)[e]
) iff
(for every
e
,
e^{+}(x\vee y)=A
) iff (for every
e
,
\{e(x)\}\cup\{e(y)\}=A
) iff
|A|=1
,
as we can take
e
such that
e(x)=e(y)
.
Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}\vDash x\wedge y
iff (for every
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(x\wedge y)[e]
) iff
(for every
e
,
e^{+}(x\wedge y)=A
) iff (for every
e
,
\{e(x)\}\cap\{e(y)\}=A
) iff
|A|=1
.

Thus,
\mathcal{A}\vDash x\vee y
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash x\wedge y
iff
|A|=1
. Thus,
x\vee y\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}x\wedge y
.

Let us prove now that
x\vee y\not\vDash_{l}x\wedge y
. Let
A=\{1,2\}
and
e
be such that
e(x)=1
and
e(y)=2
. Then

e^{+}(x\vee y)=\{e(x)\}\cup\{e(y)\}=A
. Thus,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(x\vee y)[e]
. On the other hand,

e^{+}(x\wedge y)=\{e(x)\}\cap\{e(y)\}=\emptyset
, hence
\mathcal{A}\not\vDash(x\wedge y)[e]
.

(ii)

By Proposition
3.27
,
x\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}y
. On the other hand,
x\not\vDash_{s}y
: take an
(A,e)
such that

|A|\geq 2
and
e(x)\neq e(y)
. Then
e^{+}(x)=\{e(x)\}\not\subseteq\{e(y)\}=e^{+}(y)
.

∎

3.3
Sets of patterns

Let
\Gamma
be a set of patterns.

Definition 3.29
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. We say that

e

satisfies

\Gamma
in
\mathcal{A}
if
\mathcal{A}\vDash\gamma[e]
for every
\gamma\in\Gamma
.
Notation:
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
.

Definition 3.30
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure. We say that

\mathcal{A}

satisfies

\Gamma
or that
\mathcal{A}
is a
model
of
\Gamma
if every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation satisfies
\Gamma
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Notation:
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
.

3.4
Semantic consequence and equivalence for sets of patterns

We extend to sets of patterns the notions introduced in Subsection
3.2
.

3.4.1
Notion 1

Definition 3.31
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern. We say that

\varphi
is a
global semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
\mathcal{A}
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
implies
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
.

Notation:
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
.

This is the definition of semantic consequence from
[
7
]
.
By letting
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
, we get the notion introduced in Definition

3.16
.(
i
). Hence, we write
\psi\vDash_{g}\varphi
instead of
\{\psi\}\vDash_{g}\varphi
.

Remark 3.32
.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff
\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi
is valid, then
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
.

Proof.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff (for every
\mathcal{A}
, we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
) iff
\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

Obviously, as
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
for every
\mathcal{A}
.

∎

Lemma 3.33
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,

(i)

If
\varphi\vDash_{g}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(iii)

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\wedge\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi)
.

(iv)

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi\to\varphi)
.

Proof.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi\vDash_{g}\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be a model of
\Gamma
.
As
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
, we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi
as
\varphi\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(ii)

It follows from (i) and Remark
3.17
.

(iii)

We have that

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\wedge\psi

iff

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi\wedge\psi

iff

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\wedge\psi)[e]

iff

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
and
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]

(by Proposition
3.7
.(
vii
))

iff

(for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
) and

(for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
)

iff

(for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
) and (for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi
)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(iv)

Apply (
iii
).

∎

Definition 3.34
.

Let
\Delta
be a set of patterns. We say that

(i)

\Delta
is a
semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
\mathcal{A}
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
implies
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta
.

Notation:

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\Delta
.

(ii)

\Gamma
and
\Delta
are
logic equivalent
if for every
\mathcal{A}
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta
.

Notation:

\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\Delta
.

Obviously, by letting
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we obtain the notion introduced in Definition
3.31
. Furthermore,
for
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
and
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we get the notions introduced in
Definition
3.16
.

Remark 3.35
.

\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\Delta
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\Delta
and
\Delta\vDash_{g}\Gamma
).

Lemma 3.36
.

(i)

Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\psi
.
Then
\Delta\vDash_{g}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(ii)

Assume that
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\psi
.
Then
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff
\Delta\vDash_{g}\psi
.

Proof.

(i)

We have that

\Delta\vDash_{g}\varphi

iff

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Delta
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi

iff

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Delta
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi
(as
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\psi
)

implies

for every model
\mathcal{A}
of
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi
(as
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\Delta
)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi
.

(ii)

Apply (
i
).

∎

Proposition 3.37

(Finite sets)
.

Let
\Gamma=\{\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}\}
be a finite set of patterns. Then
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{g}\{\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}\}
.

Proof.

Applying Proposition
3.15
.(
ii
), we get that for
every
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi_{i}
for every
i=1,\ldots,n
iff

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}
.
∎

3.4.2
Notion 2

Definition 3.38
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern. We say that
\varphi
is a
local semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
implies
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.

Notation:
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
.

By letting
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
, we get the notion introduced in Definition
3.19
.
Hence, we write
\psi\vDash_{l}\varphi
instead of
\{\psi\}\vDash_{l}\varphi
.

Remark 3.39
.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff
\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi
is valid, then
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
.

Proof.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff (for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
, we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
) iff (for every
\mathcal{A}
, we have that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
) iff
\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

Obviously, as
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
.

∎

Lemma 3.40
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,

(i)

If
\varphi\vDash_{l}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi
.

(iii)

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\wedge\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi)
.

(iv)

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi\to\varphi)
.

Proof.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi\vDash_{l}\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
.
Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
be such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
. Since
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
, we get that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
. Since
\varphi\vDash_{l}\psi
, it follows that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
.

(ii)

It follows from (i) and Remark
3.20
.

(iii)

We have that

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\wedge\psi

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
we have that

\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\wedge\psi)[e]

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
and

\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]

(by Proposition
3.7
.(
vii
))

iff

(for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
) and

(for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
we have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi
.

(iv)

Apply (
iii
).

∎

Definition 3.41
.

Let
\Delta
be a set of patterns. We say that

(i)

\Delta
is a
local semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
implies
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta[e]
.

Notation:
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\Delta
.

(ii)

\Gamma
and
\Delta
are
locally logically equivalent
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta[e]
.

Notation:
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\Delta
.

Obviously, by letting
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we obtain the notion introduced in Definition
3.38
. Furthermore,
for
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
and
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we get the notions introduced in
Definition
3.19
.

Remark 3.42
.

\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\Delta
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\Delta
and
\Delta\vDash_{l}\Gamma
).

Lemma 3.43
.

(i)

Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\psi
.
Then
\Delta\vDash_{l}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi
.

(ii)

Assume that
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\psi
.
Then
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff
\Delta\vDash_{l}\psi
.

Proof.

(i)

We have that

\Delta\vDash_{l}\varphi

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta[e]
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Delta[e]
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
(as
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\psi
)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
(as
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\Delta
)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi
.

(ii)

Apply (
i
).

∎

Proposition 3.44

(Finite sets)
.

Let
\Gamma=\{\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}\}
be a set of patterns. Then
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\{\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}\}
.

Proof.

Applying Proposition
3.15
.(
i
), we get that for
every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi_{i}[e]
for every
i=1,\ldots,n
iff

\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n})[e]
.
∎

3.4.3
Notion 3

Definition 3.45
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern. We say that
\varphi
is a
strong semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
.

Notation:
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
.

By letting
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
, we get the notion introduced in Definition
3.22
.

Remark 3.46
.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{s}\varphi
iff
\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi
is valid, then
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
.

Proof.

(i)

\emptyset\vDash_{s}\varphi
iff (for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
, we have that
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\emptyset}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
)
iff (for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
, we have that
A\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
) iff (for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
, we have that
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
) iff

\vDash\varphi
.

(ii)

Obviously, as
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
.

∎

Remark 3.47
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,

(i)

If
\varphi\vDash_{s}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(ii)

If
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\psi
, then
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(iii)

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi)
.

(iv)

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi\to\varphi)
.

Proof.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi\vDash_{s}\psi
and that
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
. Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
.
Then
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
, hence
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(ii)

It follows from (i) and Remark
3.23
.

(iii)

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi\wedge\psi)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\psi)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
and

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
and

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(iv)

Apply (
iii
).

∎

Definition 3.48
.

Let
\Delta
be a set of patterns. We say that

(i)

\Delta
is a
strong semantic consequence
of
\Gamma
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq\bigcap_{\delta\in\Delta}e^{+}(\delta).

Notation:
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\Delta
.

(ii)

\Gamma
and
\Delta
are
strongly logically equivalent
if for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)=\bigcap_{\delta\in\Delta}e^{+}(\delta)
.

Notation:
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\Delta
.

Obviously, by letting
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we obtain the notion introduced in Definition
3.45
. Furthermore,
for
\Gamma=\{\psi\}
and
\Delta=\{\varphi\}
, we get the notions introduced in
Definition
3.22
.

Remark 3.49
.

\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\Delta
iff (
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\Delta
and
\Delta\vDash_{s}\Gamma
).

Lemma 3.50
.

(i)

Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\psi
.
Then
\Delta\vDash_{s}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(ii)

Assume that
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\Delta
and that
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\psi
.
Then
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
iff
\Delta\vDash_{s}\psi
.

Proof.

(i)

We have that

\Delta\vDash_{s}\varphi

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Delta}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Delta}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
(as
\varphi\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\psi
)

iff

for every
(\mathcal{A},e)

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
(as
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\Delta
)

iff

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(ii)

Apply (
i
).

∎

Proposition 3.51
.

Let
\Gamma=\{\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}\}
be a finite set of patterns. Then
\Gamma\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\{\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}\}
.

Proof.

For every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\displaystyle\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}e^{+}(\varphi_{i})=e^{+}(\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}).

∎

3.4.4
Relations between the three notions

Proposition 3.52
.

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
.

Proof.

\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
:
Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
be such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
, so
e^{+}(\gamma)=A
for every
\gamma\in\Gamma
.
Hence,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)=A
. As
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
, it follows that
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
, that is

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
implies
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
: Assume that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
and let
e
be arbitrary. Then

\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
. Applying now the fact that
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
, we get that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.

∎

3.5
Closed patterns

Definition 3.53
.

A pattern
\varphi
is said to be a
closed pattern
if
FV(\varphi)=\emptyset
,
that is
\varphi
does not have free variables.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure.

Proposition 3.54
.

Let
\varphi
be a closed pattern. For every
\mathcal{A}
-valuations
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
, we have that
e_{1}^{+}(\varphi)=e_{2}^{+}(\varphi)
. In particular,

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e_{1}]
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e_{2}]
.

Proof.

Apply Proposition
3.8
and the fact that
FV(\varphi)=\emptyset
.
∎

Corollary 3.55
.

Let
\varphi
be a closed pattern. Then either
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
for all
e
or
\mathcal{A}\not\vDash\varphi[e]
for all
e
.
Hence,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
for one
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
.

Proposition 3.56
.

Let
\Gamma
be a set of closed patterns. Then for every pattern
\varphi
,
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
.

Proof.

\Rightarrow
By Proposition
3.52
.

\Leftarrow
Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
be such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
, that is
\mathcal{A}\vDash\gamma[e]
for all

\gamma\in\Gamma
. By Corollary
3.55
, we get that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\gamma
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
, that is
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
. As
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
, it follows that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi
. In particular,
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.
∎

3.6
Predicates

Definition 3.57
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern.

(i)

If
\mathcal{A}
is a
\tau
-structure, we say that
\varphi
is an
\mathcal{A}
-predicate
or a
predicate in
A
if for any

\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
e^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
.

(ii)

\varphi
is a
predicate
iff it is an
\mathcal{A}
-predicate for every
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
.

We shall denote by
\mathcal{A}
-Predicates
the set of
\mathcal{A}
-predicates and by
Predicates

the set of predicates.

Proposition 3.58
.

(i)

For every
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
, the set
\mathcal{A}
-Predicates contains
\bot
,
\top
and is
closed to
\neg,\to,\vee,\wedge,\leftrightarrow
and to
\exists x
,
\forall x
(for any element variable
x
). Furthermore, the set
\mathcal{A}
-Predicates
is closed to finite conjunctions and disjunctions.

(ii)

The set Predicates contains
\bot
,
\top
and is closed to
\neg,\to,\vee,\wedge,\leftrightarrow
and
to
\exists x
,
\forall x
(for any variable
x
). Furthermore, the set Predicates
is closed to finite conjunctions and disjunctions.

Proof.

(i)

Let
e
be an arbitrary
\mathcal{A}
-valuation.

(a)

Since
e^{+}(\bot)=\emptyset
and
e^{+}(\top)=A
, we have that

\bot,\top\in\mathcal{A}
-Predicates.

(b)

If
\varphi
is an
\mathcal{A}
-predicate, then
e^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
, hence

e^{+}(\neg\varphi)=A\setminus e^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
, that is
\neg\varphi
is an
\mathcal{A}
-predicate.

(c)

Assume that
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{n}(n\geq 1)
are
\mathcal{A}
-predicates, so
e^{+}(\varphi_{i})\in\{\emptyset,A\}
for every
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}e^{+}(\varphi_{i})=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\text{if there exists~}i=1,\ldots,n\text{~with~}e^{+}(\varphi_{i})=\emptyset\\
A&\text{otherwise},\\
\end{cases}

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi_{1}\vee\ldots\vee\varphi_{n})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}e^{+}(\varphi_{i})=\begin{cases}A&\text{if there exists~}i=1,\ldots,n\text{~with~}e^{+}(\varphi_{i})=A,\\
\emptyset&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus,
\varphi_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\varphi_{n}
and
\varphi_{1}\vee\ldots\vee\varphi_{n}
are
\mathcal{A}
-predicates.

(d)

Assume that
\varphi
,
\psi
are
\mathcal{A}
-predicates, so
e^{+}(\varphi),e^{+}(\psi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
.
Then
\varphi\wedge\psi
and
\varphi\vee\psi
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi\rightarrow\psi)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\text{if~}e^{+}(\varphi)=A\text{~and~}e^{+}(\psi)=\emptyset\\
A&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}A&\text{if~}e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\psi)\\
&\emptyset\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus,
\varphi\rightarrow\psi,\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
are
\mathcal{A}
-predicates.

(e)

Assume that
\varphi
is an
\mathcal{A}
-predicate. Then, for every
a\in A
,
\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
,
hence
e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
and

e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi)=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\in\{\emptyset,A\}
. Thus,
\exists x.\varphi
and
\forall x.\varphi
are

\mathcal{A}
-predicates.

(ii)

Obviously, by (
i
).

∎

Proposition 3.59
.

Let
\Gamma
be a set of closed predicates (that is, closed patterns that are predicates).
Then for every pattern
\varphi
,
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
.

Proof.

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
: By Proposition
3.56
.

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
iff
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
:
\Leftarrow
By Proposition
3.52
.

\Rightarrow
Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. We have the following cases:

(i)

There exists
\gamma_{0}\in\Gamma
such that
e^{+}(\gamma_{0})\neq A
. Then
e^{+}(\gamma_{0})=\emptyset
, so

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)=\emptyset\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
. Thus,
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
.

(ii)

e^{+}(\gamma)=A
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma
, hence
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
. Since
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
, we get that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
, that is
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
. We have got that
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)=A=e^{+}(\varphi)
.
Thus,
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
.

∎

Proposition 3.60
.

Let
\Gamma
be a set of patterns,
\sigma
be a predicate pattern and
\varphi
,
\psi
be patterns such
that

\varphi\vDash_{l}\psi
.

Then

(i)

\sigma\to\varphi\vDash_{l}\sigma\to\psi
;

(ii)

\sigma\to\varphi\vDash_{g}\sigma\to\psi
;

(iii)

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\sigma\to\varphi
implies

\Gamma\vDash_{l}\sigma\to\psi
;

(iv)

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\sigma\to\varphi
implies

\Gamma\vDash_{g}\sigma\to\psi
.

Proof.

(i)

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
be such that

\mathcal{A}\vDash(\sigma\to\varphi)[e]
, hence

(*)\qquad e^{+}(\sigma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi).

We have to prove that
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\sigma\to\psi)[e]
, hence that

(**)\qquad e^{+}(\sigma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi).

We have two cases:

(a)

e^{+}(\sigma)=\emptyset
. Then obviously,
(**)
holds.

(b)

e^{+}(\sigma)=A
. Then, by
(*)
,
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
, so
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.
As
\varphi\vDash_{l}\psi
, we must have that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
, that is
e^{+}(\psi)=A
. Thus,

(**)
holds.

(ii)

Apply (
i
) and the fact that
\sigma\to\varphi\vDash_{l}\sigma\to\psi

implies
\sigma\to\varphi\vDash_{g}\sigma\to\psi
,
by Proposition
3.26
.(
i
).

(iii)

Apply (
i
) and Lemma
3.40
.(
i
).

(iv)

Apply (
ii
) and Lemma
3.33
.(
i
).

∎

3.7
Tautologies

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure.

Definition 3.61
.

A propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation is a mapping
F:Pattern\to 2^{A}

that satisfies for any patterns
\varphi,\psi
,

(i)

F(\bot)=\emptyset
.

(ii)

F(\varphi\to\psi)=C_{A}(F(\varphi)\setminus F(\psi))
.

Lemma 3.62
.

Let
F
be a propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation. For any patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,

(i)

F(\varphi\to\psi)=C_{A}F(\varphi)\cup F(\psi)
.

(ii)

F(\top)=A
.

(iii)

F(\neg\varphi)=C_{A}F(\varphi)
.

(iv)

F(\varphi\vee\psi)=F(\varphi)\cup F(\psi)
.

(v)

F(\varphi\wedge\psi)=F(\varphi)\cap F(\psi)
.

(vi)

F(\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)=C_{A}(F(\varphi)\Delta F(\psi))
.

Proof.

Replace
e^{+}
with
F
in the proof of Proposition
3.5
.
∎

Lemma 3.63
.

Let
F
be a propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation. For any patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,

(i)

F(\varphi\to\psi)=A
iff
F(\varphi)\subseteq F(\psi)
.

(ii)

F(\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)=A
iff
F(\varphi)=F(\psi)
.

Proof.

Replace
e^{+}
with
F
in the proof of Proposition
3.7
.(
ix
), (
x
).
∎

Proposition 3.64
.

Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. The mapping

V_{e,\mathcal{A}}:Pattern\to 2^{A},\quad V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)

is a propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation.

Proof.

We have that

(i)

V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\bot)=e^{+}(\bot)=\emptyset

(ii)

V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\varphi\to\psi)=e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)=C_{A}(e^{+}(\varphi)\setminus e^{+}(\psi))=C_{A}(V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\varphi)\setminus V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\psi)).

∎

Definition 3.65
.

A pattern
\varphi
is a
tautology
if
F(\varphi)=A
for any
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
and
any propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation
F
.

Notation:
\vDash^{t}\varphi
.

Proposition 3.66
.

Any tautology is valid.

Proof.

Assume that
\varphi
is a tautology and let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Since, by Proposition
3.64
,
V_{e,\mathcal{A}}
is a propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation, we have that

V_{e,\mathcal{A}}(\varphi)=A
, that is
e^{+}(\varphi)=A
, so
\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
.
∎

3.7.1
Examples of tautologies and tautological equivalences

Proposition 3.67
.

The following are tautologies, hence valid, for all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,
\chi
,

\displaystyle\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi,

(6)

\displaystyle\varphi\vee\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi,

(7)

\displaystyle\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi\wedge\varphi,

(8)

\displaystyle\varphi\vee\psi\leftrightarrow\psi\vee\varphi,

(9)

\displaystyle\varphi\wedge\psi\leftrightarrow\psi\wedge\varphi,

(10)

\displaystyle\varphi\to\varphi\vee\psi,

(11)

\displaystyle\varphi\wedge\psi\to\varphi,

(12)

\displaystyle\bot\to\varphi,

(13)

\displaystyle\varphi\vee\neg\varphi,

(14)

\displaystyle(\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi))\leftrightarrow(\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi),

(15)

\displaystyle(\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi))\leftrightarrow(\psi\to(\varphi\to\chi)),

(16)

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure and
F
be an propositional
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation.

(
6
): We have that
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
, hence,
by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi)=A
.

(
7
): We have that
F(\varphi\vee\varphi)=F(\varphi)\cup F(\varphi)=F(\varphi)
,
hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\vee\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi)=A
.

(
8
): We have that
F(\varphi\wedge\varphi)=F(\varphi)\cap F(\varphi)=F(\varphi)
,
hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi\wedge\varphi)=A
.

(
9
): We have that
F(\varphi\vee\psi)=F(\varphi)\cup F(\psi)=F(\psi)\cup F(\varphi)=F(\psi\vee\varphi)
, hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\vee\psi\leftrightarrow\psi\vee\varphi)=A
.

(
10
): We have that
F(\varphi\wedge\psi)=F(\varphi)\cap F(\psi)=F(\psi)\cap F(\varphi)=F(\psi\wedge\varphi)
, hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\wedge\psi\leftrightarrow\psi\wedge\varphi)=A
.

(
11
): We have that
F(\varphi)\subseteq F(\varphi)\cup F(\psi)=F(\varphi\vee\psi)
,
hence,
by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\to\varphi\vee\psi)=A
.

(
12
): We have that
F(\varphi\wedge\psi)=F(\varphi)\cap F(\psi)\subseteq F(\varphi)
,
hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F(\varphi\wedge\psi\to\varphi)=A
.

(
13
): We have that
F(\bot\to\varphi)=C_{A}(F(\bot)\setminus F(\varphi))=C_{A}(\emptyset\setminus F(\varphi))=C_{A}\emptyset=A
.

(
14
): We have that

F(\varphi\vee\neg\varphi)=F(\varphi)\cup F(\neg\varphi)=F(\varphi)\cup C_{A}F(\varphi)=A
.

(
15
): We have that

\displaystyle F(\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi))

\displaystyle=C_{A}F(\varphi)\cup F(\psi\to\chi)=C_{A}F(\varphi)\cup(C_{A}F(\psi)\cup F(\chi))

\displaystyle=(C_{A}F(\varphi)\cup C_{A}F(\psi))\cup F(\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{de-morgan-2}}}{{=}}C_{A}(F(\varphi)\cap F(\psi))\cup F(\chi)

\displaystyle=C_{A}F(\varphi\wedge\psi)\cup F(\chi)=F(\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi),

hence, by Lemma
3.63
,
F((\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi))\leftrightarrow(\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi))=A
.

(
16
): We have that

\displaystyle F(\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{exp+imp}}}{{=}}F(\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi)=C_{A}F(\varphi\wedge\psi)\cup F(\chi)

\displaystyle=C_{A}F(\psi\wedge\varphi)\cup F(\chi)\quad\text{as }F(\varphi\wedge\psi)=F(\psi\wedge\varphi)\text{ by \eqref{dnd-permutation-si}}

\displaystyle=F(\psi\wedge\varphi\to\chi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{exp+imp}}}{{=}}F(\psi\to(\varphi\to\chi)).

∎

3.8
Change of
\mathcal{A}
-valuations

Notation 3.68
.

For any distinct element variables
x,z
and any
a,b\in A
, we define a new
\mathcal{A}
-valuation

e_{x\mapsto{a},z\mapsto{b}}
as follows: for all
v\in EVar
and all
V\in SVar
,

\displaystyle e_{x\mapsto{a},z\mapsto{b}}(V)=e(V),

\displaystyle\qquad e_{x\mapsto{a},z\mapsto{b}}(v)=\begin{cases}e(v)&\text{ if }v\neq x,z\\
a&\text{~if~}v=x\\
b&\text{~if~}v=z\end{cases}

(17)

It is obvious that
e_{x\mapsto{a},z\mapsto{b}}=\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{z\mapsto{b}}=\left(e_{z\mapsto{b}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}
.

Notation 3.69
.

For any distinct set variables
X,Z
and any
B,C\subseteq A
, we define a new
\mathcal{A}
-valuation

e_{X\mapsto{B},Z\mapsto{C}}
as follows: for all
v\in EVar
and all
V\in SVar
,

\displaystyle e_{X\mapsto{B},Z\mapsto{C}}(v)=e(v),

\displaystyle\qquad e_{X\mapsto{B},Z\mapsto{C}}(V)=\begin{cases}e(V)&\text{~if~}V\neq X,Z\\
B&\text{~if~}V=X\\
C&\text{~if~}V=Z\end{cases}

(18)

It is obvious that
e_{X\mapsto{B},Z\mapsto{C}}=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{C}}=\left(e_{Z\mapsto{C}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}
.

Notation 3.70
.

For any element variable
x
and set variable
X
and any
a\in A,B\subseteq A
, we define a new
\mathcal{A}
-valuation

e_{x\mapsto{a},X\mapsto{B}}
as follows: for all
v\in EVar
and all
V\in SVar
,

\displaystyle e_{x\mapsto{a},X\mapsto{B}}(v)=\begin{cases}e(v)&\text{~if~}v\neq x\\
a&\text{~if~}v=x\end{cases}

\displaystyle\qquad e_{x\mapsto{a},X\mapsto{B}}(V)=\begin{cases}e(V)&\text{~if~}V\neq X\\
B&\text{~if~}V=X\end{cases}

(19)

It is obvious that
e_{x\mapsto{a},X\mapsto{b}}=\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}
.

3.9
Substitution

3.9.1
Element variables

Proposition 3.71
.

Let
\varphi
,
\delta
be patterns and
x
be an element variable such that
x
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
. Assume that

(*)
\mathcal{A}
is a
\tau
-structure such that for every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
, there exists
c_{e}\in A
such that
e^{+}(\delta)=\{c_{e}\}
.

Then for every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,

e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

Proof.

If
x\notin FV(\varphi)
, then
Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi=\varphi
and
e=e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}
, by Proposition
3.8
.
The conclusion is obvious.

We prove by induction on patterns that for all patterns
\varphi
such that
x\in FV(\varphi)
, the following holds:

for every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)
.

We use the definition by recursion on paterns of
Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi
, given by Remark
2.30
.

(i)

\varphi
is an atomic pattern. Since
x\in FV(\varphi)
, we have that
\varphi=x\in EVar
. Then
Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi=\delta
.
For every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
, we get that
e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)=e^{+}(\delta)=\{c_{e}\}
. Furthermore,

\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(x)=\{c_{e}\}
.

(ii)

\varphi={\psi}\cdot{\chi}
. Then
x\in FV(\psi)
,
x\in FV(\chi)
and
x
is free for

\delta
in
\psi
,
\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
and
\chi
to get that

e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)
and
e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi})=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi}}\cdot{Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi}})=e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})\star e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi})

\displaystyle=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

(iii)

\varphi=\psi\to\chi
. Then
x\in FV(\psi)
,
x\in FV(\chi)
and
x
is free for

\delta
in
\psi
,
\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
and
\chi
to get that

e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)
and
e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi})=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi}\to Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi})=C_{A}\left(e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})\setminus e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\chi})\right)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\setminus\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\chi)\right)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

(iv)

\varphi=\exists z.\psi
. Since
x\in FV(\varphi)
, we have that
x\neq z
.
Then
x\in FV(\psi)
and
x
is free for
\delta
in
\psi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We get that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\exists z.Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{z\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{z\mapsto{a}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e_{z\mapsto a}}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{z\mapsto a}.

As
x
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
and
x\in FV(\psi)
, it follows that

z\notin FV(\delta)
. Hence,
by Proposition
3.8
,
e^{+}(\delta)=\left(e_{z\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\delta)
, so

c_{e}=c_{e_{z\mapsto a}}
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{z\mapsto{a}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto c_{e},z\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)_{z\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi),

since
x\neq z
, hence
\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)_{z\mapsto{a}}=\left(e_{z\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}=e_{x\mapsto c_{e},z\mapsto a}
.

(v)

\varphi=\mu X.\psi
. Then
x\in FV(\psi)
and
x
is free for
\delta
in
\psi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation.

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\mu X.Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}{\psi})\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e_{X\mapsto{B}}}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{X\mapsto{B}}.

As
x
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
and
x\in FV(\psi)
, it follows that

X\notin FV(\delta)
. Hence,
by Proposition
3.8
,
e^{+}(\delta)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\delta)
, so

c_{e}=c_{e_{X\mapsto{B}}}
. Thus,
\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e_{X\mapsto{B}}}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(e_{x\mapsto{c_{e}}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

∎

As an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.71
, we get the following.

Corollary 3.72
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
x,y
be variables such that
x
is free for
y
in
\varphi
.
Then for every
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,

e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{y}\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(y)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

Proposition 3.73
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
x
,
y
be variables such that
x
is free for
y
in
\varphi
. Then

\displaystyle\vDash Subf^{x}_{y}\varphi\to\exists x.\varphi,

(20)

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\to Subf^{x}_{y}\varphi.

(21)

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Apply Corollary
3.72
to get that

e^{+}(Subf^{x}_{y}\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(y)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)
. It follows that

(
22
):
e^{+}(\varphi_{x}(y))=\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(y)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi)
.

(
23
):
e^{+}(\varphi_{x}(y))=\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(y)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\supseteq\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi)
.
∎

Bounded substitution

Proposition 3.74
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
x,y
variables such that
y
does not occur in
\varphi
. Then

\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi.

Proof.

If
x=y
, then obviously
Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi=\varphi
, hence
\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi
. Assume that
x\neq y
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure. We prove by induction on
\varphi
that

for every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)
.

We use the definition by recursion on paterns of
Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi
, given by Remark
2.37
.

(i)

\varphi
is an atomic pattern. Then
Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi=\varphi
, so
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)
for every

\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
.

(ii)

\varphi={\psi}\cdot{\chi}
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
,
\chi
, we have
that
e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})
and
e^{+}(\chi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\chi})
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({Subb^{x}_{y}(\psi)}\cdot{Subb^{x}_{y}(\chi)})=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})\star e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\chi})=e^{+}(\psi)\star e^{+}(\chi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})=e^{+}(\varphi).

(iii)

\varphi=\psi\to\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
,
\chi
, we have
that
e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})
and
e^{+}(\chi)=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\chi})
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\to Subb^{x}_{y}{\chi})=C_{A}e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})\cup e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\chi})=C_{A}e^{+}(\psi)\cup e^{+}(\chi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\psi\to\chi)=e^{+}(\varphi).

(iv)

\varphi=\exists z.\psi
. We have two cases:

1.
x\neq z
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\exists z.Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{z\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{z\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{z\mapsto a}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

2.
x=z
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}\left(\exists y.Subf^{x}_{y}{\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)}\right)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{y\mapsto a}\right)^{+}\left(Subf^{x}_{y}{\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)}\right).

As
x\neq y
and
y
does not occur in
\psi
, we have that
x
is free for

y
in
Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}
, by Proposition
2.39
. We can apply then Corollary
3.72
to get that

\displaystyle\left(e_{y\mapsto a}\right)^{+}\left(Subf^{x}_{y}{\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{y\mapsto{a}}\right)_{x\mapsto{e_{y\mapsto a}(y)}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)=\left(\left(e_{y\mapsto{a}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{y\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)=\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right),

since
y
does not occur in
\psi
, so
y\notin FV\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)
, hence we can apply
Proposition
3.8
.
It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{x\mapsto a}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

(v)

\varphi=\mu X.\psi
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\mu X.Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(Subb^{x}_{y}{\psi})\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{X\mapsto{B}}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

∎

Free substitution

Proposition 3.75
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
x
,
y
be variables. Then

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi_{x}(y)\to\exists x.\varphi,

(22)

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\to\varphi_{x}(y).

(23)

Proof.

We have the following two cases:

(i)

x
is free for
y
in
\varphi
. Then
\varphi_{x}(y)=Subf^{x}_{y}\varphi
, so we apply Corollary
3.73

to get the conclusion.

(ii)

x
is not free for
y
in
\varphi
. Then
\varphi_{x}(y)=Subf^{x}_{y}{\theta}
, where
\theta=Subb^{y}_{z}{\varphi}
,
with
z
a new variable,
z\notin Var(\varphi)\cup\{y\}
. Since
x
is free for
y
in
\theta
, we can apply
Corollary
3.73
(with
\varphi=\theta
)
to get that

\vDash\varphi_{x}(y)\to\exists x.\theta\text{~and~}\vDash\forall x.\theta\to\varphi_{x}(y).

(24)

Furthermore, by Proposition
3.74
, we have that

\vDash\theta\leftrightarrow\varphi.

(25)

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi_{x}(y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fs-1}}}{{\subseteq}}e^{+}(\exists x.\theta)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\theta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fs-2}}}{{=}}\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi),

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi_{x}(y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fs-1}}}{{\supseteq}}e^{+}(\forall x.\theta)=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\theta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{fs-2}}}{{=}}\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi),

Hence,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi_{x}(y)\to\exists x.\varphi)[e]
and
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\forall x.\varphi\to\varphi_{x}(y))[e]
.

∎

3.9.2
Set variables

Proposition 3.76
.

Let
\varphi
,
\delta
be patterns and
X
be a set variable such that
X
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
.
Then for every
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,

e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

Proof.

If
X\notin FV(\varphi)
, then
Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi=\varphi
and
e=e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}
, so the
conclusion is obvious.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure.
We prove by induction on patterns that for all patterns
\varphi
such that
X\in FV(\varphi)
, the following holds:

for every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)
.

We use the definition by recursion on paterns of
Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi
, given by Remark
2.41
.

Assume in the sequel that
X\in FV(\varphi)
. The proof is by induction on
\varphi
.

(i)

\varphi
is an atomic pattern. Since
X\in FV(\varphi)
, we have that
\varphi=X
. Then
Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi=\delta
.
For every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
, we get that
e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)=e^{+}(\delta)
. Furthermore,

\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(X)=e^{+}(\delta)
.

(ii)

\varphi={\psi}\cdot{\chi}
. Then
X\in FV(\psi)
,
X\in FV(\chi)
and
X
is free for

\delta
in
\psi
,
\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
and
\chi
to get that

e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)
and
e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi}})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({{Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}}}\cdot{{Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi}}})=e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}})\star e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi}})

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

(iii)

\varphi=\psi\to\chi
. Then
X\in FV(\psi)
,
X\in FV(\chi)
and
X
is free for

\delta
in
\psi
,
\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
and
\chi
to get that

e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)
and
e^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi}})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}\to Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi})=C_{A}e^{+}Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}\cup e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\chi})

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cup\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

(iv)

\varphi=\exists x.\psi
. Then
X\in FV(\psi)
and
X
is free for
\delta
in
\psi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation.

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\exists x.Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi})=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi})

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{x\mapsto a}.

As
X
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
and
X\in FV(\psi)
, it follows that

x\notin FV(\delta)
. Hence,
by Proposition
3.8
,
e^{+}(\delta)=\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\delta)
.

It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\exists x.\psi)

(v)

\varphi=\mu Z.\psi
. Since
X\in FV(\varphi)
, we have that
X\neq Z
.
Then
X\in FV(\psi)
and
X
is free for
\delta
in
\psi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. We get that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\mu Z.{Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}})=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\delta}{\psi}})\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{Z\mapsto{B}}.

As
X
is free for
\delta
in
\varphi
and
X\in FV(\varphi)
, it follows that
Z\notin FV(\delta)
. Hence,
by Proposition
3.8
,
e^{+}(\delta)=\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\delta)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{\delta}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\mu Z.\psi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\delta)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

∎

As an immediate consequence of Proposition
3.76
, we get the following.

Corollary 3.77
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
X,Y
be variables such that
X
is free for
Y
in
\varphi
.
Then for every
\tau
-structure
\mathcal{A}
and every
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,

e^{+}(Subf^{X}_{Y}\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{e(Y)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

Bounded substitution

Proposition 3.78
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
X,Y
variables such that
Y
does not occur in
\varphi
. Then

\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi.

Proof.

If
X=Y
, then obviously
Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi=\varphi
, hence
\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi
. Assume that
X\neq Y
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a
\tau
-structure. We prove by induction on
\varphi
that

for any
\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
,
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)
.

We use the definition by recursion on paterns of
Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi
given by Remark
2.46
.

(i)

\varphi
is an atomic pattern. Then
Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi=\varphi
, hence
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)
for every

\mathcal{A}
-valuation
e
.

(ii)

\varphi={\psi}\cdot{\chi}
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
,
\chi
, we have
that

e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})
and
e^{+}(\chi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\chi})
.
It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({Subb^{X}_{Y}(\psi)}\cdot{Subb^{X}_{Y}(\chi)})=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})\star e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})=e^{+}(\psi)\star e^{+}(\chi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})=e^{+}(\varphi).

(iii)

\varphi=\psi\to\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-valuation. By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
,
\chi
, we have
that
e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})
and
e^{+}(\chi)=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\chi})
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\to Subb^{X}_{Y}{\chi})=C_{A}e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})\cup e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\chi})

\displaystyle=C_{A}e^{+}(\psi)\cup e^{+}(\chi)=e^{+}(\psi\to\chi)=e^{+}(\varphi).

(iv)

\varphi=\exists x.\psi
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\exists x.Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\quad\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{x\mapsto a}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

(v)

\varphi=\mu Z.\psi
. We have two cases:

1.
X\neq Z
, then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\mu Z.Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi})\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{Z\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{Z\mapsto{B}}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

2.
X=Z
. Then

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}\left(\mu Y.{Subf^{X}_{Y}{\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{Y\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{Y}{\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)}})\subseteq B\right\}.

As
Y
does not occur in
\psi
, we have that that
X
is free for

Y
in
Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}
, by Proposition
2.48
. We can apply then Corollary
3.77
to get that

\displaystyle\left(e_{Y\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}\left({Subf^{X}_{Y}{\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{Y\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{e_{Y\mapsto{B}}(Y)}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{Y\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)

since
Y
does not occur in
\psi
, so
Y\notin FV\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)
, hence we can apply
Proposition
3.8
. It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(Subb^{X}_{Y}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}\left(Subb^{X}_{Y}{\psi}\right)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}\left(\psi\right)\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle\text{ by the induction hypothesis for }\psi\text{ applied to }e_{X\mapsto{B}}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi).

∎

3.10
Semantics of
\nu

Proposition 3.79
.

For every pattern
\varphi
and set variable
X
,

e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi)=\bigcup\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\supseteq D\right\}.

Proof.

As
\nu X.\varphi=\neg\mu X.\neg{Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}}
, we get that

\displaystyle e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\neg\mu X.\neg{Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}})=C_{A}e^{+}(\mu X.\neg{Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=C_{A}\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\neg{Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}}))\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=C_{A}\bigcap\left\{B\subseteq A\mid C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}}))\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\left\{C_{A}B\mid B\subseteq A\text{~such that~}C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}}))\subseteq B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\left\{C_{A}B\mid B\subseteq A\text{~such that~}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}}))\supseteq C_{A}B\right\}.

As, obviously,
X
is free for
\neg X
in
\varphi
, we have by Proposition
3.76
that

\displaystyle\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({Subf^{X}_{\neg X}{\varphi}})

\displaystyle=\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\neg X)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{X\mapsto{C_{A}B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C_{A}B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

It follows that

\displaystyle e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup\left\{C_{A}B\mid B\subseteq A\text{~such that~}\left(e_{X\mapsto{C_{A}B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\supseteq C_{A}B\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\supseteq D\right\}.

∎

3.11
Least and greatest fixpoints

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
X
be a set variable. Define, for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
, the functions

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}:2^{A}\to 2^{A},

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi),

(26)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}:2^{A}\to 2^{A},

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=C_{A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi).

(27)

Proposition 3.80
.

(i)

If
\varphi
is positive in
X
, then for all
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}
is monotone.

(ii)

If
\varphi
is negative in
X
, then for all
(\mathcal{A},e)
,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}
is monotone.

Proof.

We prove (
i
) and (
ii
) simultaneously
by induction on
\varphi
. We use Remarks
2.27
,
2.28
. Let
B,C\subseteq A
be such that
B\subseteq C
.

(a)

\varphi
is atomic and
\varphi\neq X
. Then
\varphi
is both positive and negative in
X
. As
X\notin FV(\varphi)
,
we have that
\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
. It follows that

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)=e^{+}(\varphi)
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)=C_{A}e^{+}(\varphi)
.

(b)

\varphi=X
. Then
\varphi
is positive in
X
, hence

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(X)=B\subseteq C=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(X)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(c)

\varphi={\psi}\cdot{\chi}
.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi
is positive in
X
. We have that both
\psi
,
\chi
are positive in
X
.
We can apply the induction hypothesis to get that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\star\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{B-se-C-appls-D-se E}}}{{\subseteq}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\star\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(ii)

Assume that
\varphi
is negative in
X
. We have that both
\psi
,
\chi
are negative in
X
.
We can apply the induction hypothesis to get that
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
and

\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)
, hence
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\supseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
and

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)\supseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\star\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{B-se-C-appls-D-se E}}}{{\supseteq}}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\star\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\star\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}({\psi}\cdot{\chi})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

Thus,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)
.

(d)

\varphi=\psi\to\chi
.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi
is positive in
X
. We have that
\psi
is negative in
X
and
\chi
is positive in
X
.
We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
to get that

\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
and the induction hypothesis for
\chi
to get that

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cup\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\chi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\cup\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\cup\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cup\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(ii)

Assume that
\varphi
is negative in
X
. We have that
\psi
is positive in
X
and
\chi
is negative in
X
.
We can apply the induction hypothesis for
\psi
to get that

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
and the induction hypothesis for
\chi
to get that

\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)=C_{A}\bigg(C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cup\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\chi)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cap C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\chi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\cap\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\cap\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\chi},{X}}(C)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cap C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\chi)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\bigg(C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\cup\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\chi)\bigg)=C_{A}\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(e)

\varphi=\exists x.\psi
.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi
is positive in
X
. We have that
\psi
is positive in
X
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\exists x.\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)

\displaystyle\text{by the induction hypothesis for~}\psi\text{~applied to~}e_{x\mapsto a}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\exists x.\psi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(ii)

Assume that
\varphi
is negative in
X
. We have that
\psi
is negative in
X
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\exists x.\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\supseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)

as, by the induction hypothesis for
\psi
applied to
e_{x\mapsto a}
, we have that
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
, hence

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\supseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{x\mapsto a}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)_{x\mapsto{a}}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\exists x.\psi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

Hence,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)
.

(f)

\varphi=\mu Z.\psi
. If
Z=X
, then
\varphi
is both positive and negative in
X
. As
X\notin FV(\varphi)
,
we have that
\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
. It follows that

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)=e^{+}(\varphi)
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)=\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)=C_{A}e^{+}(\varphi)
.

Suppose that
X\neq Z
.

(i)

Assume that
\varphi
is positive in
X
. We have that
\psi
is positive in
X
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\mu Z.\psi)=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}.

By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
applied to
e_{Z\mapsto{D}}
, we have that
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
, so

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D
implies
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D
, hence

\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}\subseteq\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}.

Thus,

\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}\subseteq\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\mu Z.\psi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

(ii)

Assume that
\varphi
is negative in
X
. We have that
\psi
is negative in
X
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\mu Z.\psi)=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}.

By the induction hypothesis for
\psi
applied to
e_{Z\mapsto{D}}
, we have that

\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
, so
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\supseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)
. It follows that

\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D
implies
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D
, hence

\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}\subseteq\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}.

Thus,

\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}\subseteq\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(B)\subseteq D\right\}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e_{Z\mapsto{D}}}_{{\psi},{X}}(C)\subseteq D\right\}=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\left\{D\subseteq A\mid\left(\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)_{Z\mapsto{D}}\right)^{+}(\psi)\subseteq D\right\}

\displaystyle=\left(e_{X\mapsto{C}}\right)^{+}(\mu Z.\psi)=\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C).

Hence,
\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(C)
.

∎

As a consequence of Knaster-Tarski Theorem
B.9
, it follows that if
\varphi
is positive in
X
, then
\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}
has
a least fixpoint
\mu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}
and a greatest fixpoint
\nu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}
, given by:

\displaystyle\mu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\{B\subseteq A\mid\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)\subseteq B\}=\bigcap\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq B\}

\displaystyle=\bigcap\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=B\},

\displaystyle\nu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\{B\subseteq A\mid B\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}(B)\}=\bigcup\{B\subseteq A\mid B\subseteq\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup\{B\subseteq A\mid\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=B\}.

Furthermore, by Definition
3.4
.(
vii
) and
Proposition
3.79
, we get that

\displaystyle\mu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}=e^{+}(\mu X.\varphi)\quad\text{and}\quad\nu\mathcal{F}^{\mathcal{A},e}_{{\varphi},{X}}=e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi).

(28)

Thus,

Proposition 3.81
.

Let
\varphi
be a pattern and
X
be a set variable such that
\varphi
is positive in
X
. Then, for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
,

(i)

\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\mu X.\varphi)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\mu X.\varphi)
and
\left(e_{X\mapsto{e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi)
.

(ii)

For every
B\subseteq A
such that
\left(e_{X\mapsto{B}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=B
,

e^{+}(\mu X.\varphi)\subseteq B\subseteq e^{+}(\nu X.\varphi).

3.12
Equivalences, logical consequences, validities

Proposition 3.82
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,
\chi
,

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi,

(29)

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi\quad\text{iff}\quad\vDash\psi\leftrightarrow\varphi,

(30)

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi\text{ and }\vDash\psi\leftrightarrow\chi\quad\text{implies}\quad\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow\chi,

(31)

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi\to\psi\text{ and }\vDash\psi\to\chi\quad\text{implies}\quad\vDash\varphi\to\chi.

(32)

Proof.

(
29
): It follows by (
6
) and Proposition
3.66
.

(
30
): We have that

\vDash\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi
iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)[e]
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
iff

e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\psi)
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
iff
e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)

iff
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\psi\leftrightarrow\varphi)[e]
for every
(\mathcal{A},e)
iff
\vDash\psi\leftrightarrow\varphi
.

(
31
): Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\leftrightarrow\psi)[e]
, hence

e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\psi)
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\psi\leftrightarrow\chi)[e]
, hence

e^{+}(\psi)=e^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that
e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\chi)
. Thus,

\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\leftrightarrow\chi)[e]
.

(
32
): Assume that
\vDash\varphi\to\psi

and
\vDash\psi\to\chi
. Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\psi)[e]
, hence

e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\psi\to\chi)[e]
, hence

e^{+}(\psi)\subseteq e^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that
e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\chi)
. Thus,

\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\chi)[e]
.
∎

Proposition 3.83
.

For any pattern
\varphi
and any variable
x
,

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\to\exists x.\varphi,

(33)

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\to\varphi,

(34)

\displaystyle\vDash\varphi\to\exists x.\varphi,

(35)

\displaystyle\vDash\exists x.x,

(36)

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\text{ iff }\vDash\varphi

(37)

Proof.

We apply Proposition
3.7
.(
ix
).

(
33
): Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then

e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi)=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi)
.

(
34
): Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then

e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi)=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(x)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
.

(
35
): Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then
e^{+}(\varphi)=\left(e_{x\mapsto{e(x)}}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi)
.

(
36
): Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. Then

e^{+}(\exists x.x)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(x)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\{a\}=A
.
Thus,
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\exists x.x)[e]
.

(
37
): By Proposition
3.11
.(
viii
).
∎

Proposition 3.84
.

For any pattern
\varphi
and any variable
x
,

\displaystyle\vDash\exists x.\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi\quad\text{ if }x\notin FV(\varphi),

(38)

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi\quad\text{ if }x\notin FV(\varphi).

(39)

Proof.

We apply Proposition
3.7
.(
x
). Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
.
As
x\notin FV(\varphi)
, we can apply Proposition
3.8
to get that

\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
for any
a\in A
.

(
38
): We have that

e^{+}(\exists x.\varphi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\bigcup_{a\in A}e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
.

(
39
): We have that

e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi)=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)=\bigcap_{a\in A}e^{+}(\varphi)=e^{+}(\varphi)
.
∎

Proposition 3.85
.

For any pattern
\varphi
and any variable
x
,

\displaystyle\vDash\forall x.(\varphi\to\psi)\to(\forall x.\varphi\to\forall x.\psi).

(40)

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
. We have that

\displaystyle e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi\to\forall x.\psi)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\forall x\varphi)\cup\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\forall x\psi)\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{basic-val}.\eqref{basic-val-imp}}

\displaystyle=C_{A}\left(\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\right)\cup\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{a\in A}C_{A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\cup\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)\quad\text{by \eqref{de-morgan-family-CAcap}}

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(\bigcup_{a\in A}C_{A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\cup\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)\right)\quad\text{by \eqref{distrib-cap-family-left}}

\displaystyle\supseteq\bigcap_{a\in A}\big(C_{A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi)\cup\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\psi)\big)

\displaystyle=\bigcap_{a\in A}\left(e_{x\mapsto a}\right)^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{basic-val}.\eqref{basic-val-imp}}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\forall x.(\varphi\to\psi))\quad\text{by Lemma \ref{basic-val}.\eqref{basic-val-forall}}

We have got that
e^{+}(\forall x.(\varphi\to\psi))\subseteq e^{+}(\forall x.\varphi\to\forall x.\psi)
. Apply Proposition
3.7
.(
ix
)
to conclude that

\mathcal{A}\vDash\big(\forall x.(\varphi\to\psi)\to(\forall x.\varphi\to\forall x.\psi)\big)[e]
.
∎

3.13
Rules

Let
\Gamma
be a set of patterns.

3.13.1
\vDash_{s}

Proposition 3.86
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,
\chi
,

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi

(41)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\psi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\chi

(42)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)

(43)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi

(44)

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
.

(
41
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\psi
. Then
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)
and
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)
, hence
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)
. Remark that

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi)\cap\left(C_{A}e^{+}(\varphi)\cup e^{+}(\psi)\right)=\left(e^{+}(\varphi)\cap C_{A}e^{+}(\varphi)\right)\cup\left(e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\psi)\right)

\displaystyle=\emptyset\cup\left(e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\psi)\right)=e^{+}(\varphi)\cap e^{+}(\psi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi).

Hence,
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
, that is
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi
.

(
42
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{s}\psi\to\chi
. Then

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)
and
\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi\to\chi)
, hence

\bigcap_{\gamma\in\Gamma}e^{+}(\gamma)\subseteq e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)\cap e^{+}(\psi\to\chi)
.
Remark now that

\displaystyle e^{+}(\varphi\to\psi)\cap e^{+}(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle=C_{A}\big(e^{+}(\varphi)\setminus e^{+}(\psi)\big)\cap C_{A}\big(e^{+}(\psi)\setminus e^{+}(\chi)\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{de-morgan-1}}}{{=}}C_{A}\bigg((e^{+}(\varphi)\setminus e^{+}(\psi))\cup(e^{+}(\psi)\setminus e^{+}(\chi))\bigg)

\displaystyle\subseteq C_{A}\big(e^{+}(\varphi)\setminus e^{+}(\chi)\big)\text{~by \eqref{A-C-se-A-B-cup-B-C} and \eqref{CA-B-se-D}}

\displaystyle=e^{+}(\varphi\to\chi).

(
43
), (
44
): By (
15
) and
Remark
3.24
.(
ii
),
we have that
\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi
. Apply now
Remark
3.47
.(
ii
).
∎

3.13.2
\vDash_{l}

Proposition 3.87
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,
\chi
,

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi

(45)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\psi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\chi

(46)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)

(47)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi

(48)

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{A},e)
be such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma[e]
.

(
45
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\psi
. Then

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
and
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\psi)[e]
, hence
e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
.
It follows that

e^{+}(\psi)=A
, hence
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
.

(
46
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{l}\psi\to\chi
.
Then
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\psi)[e]
and
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\psi\to\chi)[e]
, hence

e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
and
e^{+}(\psi)\subseteq e^{+}(\chi)
. It follows that

e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\chi)
, so
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\chi)[e]
.

(
47
), (
48
):
By (
15
) and
Remark
3.24
.(
ii
),
we have that
\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{s}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi
, hence, by
Remark
3.26
.(
ii
),

\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi)\mathrel{\text{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\vDash$\cr\reflectbox{$\vDash$}\cr}}}}_{l}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi
.
Apply now
Remark
3.40
.(
ii
)
∎

3.13.3
\vDash_{g}

Proposition 3.88
.

For all patterns
\varphi
,
\psi
,
\chi
,

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi,

(49)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi\text{ and }\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\chi,

(50)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi),

(51)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to(\psi\to\chi),

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\wedge\psi\to\chi,

(52)

\displaystyle\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi

\displaystyle\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Gamma\vDash_{g}\chi\vee\varphi\to\chi\vee\psi.

(53)

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be such that
\mathcal{A}\vDash\Gamma
.

(
49
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation. Then

\mathcal{A}\vDash\varphi[e]
and
\mathcal{A}\vDash(\varphi\to\psi)[e]
, so
A=e^{+}(\varphi)\subseteq e^{+}(\psi)
. It follows that

e^{+}(\psi)=A
, hence
\mathcal{A}\vDash\psi[e]
.

(
50
): Assume that
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\varphi\to\psi
and
\Gamma\vDash_{g}\psi\to\chi
. Let
e
be an
\mathcal{A}
-evaluation.
